# Independent review: ClassicalArithmeticCompletion

Job `REV-ClassicalArithmeticCompletion`, [issue532](https://github.com/CBirkbeck/tauceti-explorer/issues/532). Codex session `codex-rtOQ9t`, 2026-10-05. Claim5986984530 was confirmed by bot5986985685. Review base `aae39157`; latest blueprint continuation was [PR6113](https://github.com/CBirkbeck/tauceti-explorer/pull/6113), by `codex-7e92bd`. This reviewer authored none of the reviewed roadmap work. Shared GitHub credentials do not establish session identity.

**Verdict: needs_changes.** This review covers every inherited node, baseline declaration, source finding and the complete suggested file. The partial stage lists are not the reason for rejection. Several *planned node contracts* still conceal nonroutine proof steps, bundle declarations at lemma granularity, consume APIs without their own suppliers, or omit essential native signatures. Clear mathematical corrections are applied below; unresolved obligations have individual node verdicts and gaps. No atlas promotion was run.

The earlier accepted reviews were scoped fixes, not acceptance of this whole packet. They remain attributed in `reviewHistory`. Author compilation and provenance receipts are preserved in `checksHistory` and `provenanceHistory`; they do not claim compilation of the current edited file.

The packet remains `status:complete` in the protocol sense of a completed pass, with precise partial stages. This does not mean its nodes are proved or independently accepted. A verified row below checks the node’s own local contract and proof sketch conditionally on its listed suppliers; it does not override an unverifiable prerequisite. The `needs_changes` verdict prevents the packet from being treated as globally closed.

## Corrections applied and remaining work

The rational finite-field root count now counts nonzero roots when the exponent is0. Cartier decimation requires q>0; power-series/polynomial preservation additionally requires r<q, with q=0 explicitly given value0. The Stern–Brocot surjectivity measure is the positive-coordinate sum U+V, rather than a false denominator/depth bound. Pairwise gcd preservation replaces prime-support reasoning in the Markoff coprimality proof. Wuthrich source locators were corrected.

The binomial-factorization example no longer calls two length-two factorizations unequal in length; a genuine lengths3/2 example is supplied. Fractionality of characteristic ideals now handles finite domains as fields. The Capelli proof separates the k=0,k=1 and k≥2 two-power branches. Egyptian splitting excludes n=1, and strict denominator ordering is restricted to inputs at most1; the input2 counterexample yields [1,1]. Numerical-semigroup gaps are a Set, and the Frobenius arithmetic uses signed subtraction. The regulator certificate divides only by the positive regulator.

Mahler degree-drop source matching retains M(α)>1. Dobrowolski’s proof now pads the auxiliary polynomial to exact degree and uses the coefficient-sum factor N+1; rounding and numerical-constant lemmas remain missing. The power-series normalization uses an even constant shift instead of the false inference U(0)=±1. Dimitrov’s cyclotomic condition is 4∤N: Φ6 gives P2=P4=Φ3. The quadratic normal-generator example excludes characteristic2; the trivial integral-basis example requires an injective algebraMap. The native unit comparison now records uniqueness.

Local-freeness rank/projectivity and completion-equality consequences exclude a field base, where height-one conditions are vacuous. The associated-order trivial test requires a Dedekind base. The rank-one representative statement requires positive total rank. Fröhlich’s pending statement uses a maximal overorder with its compatible group-ring inclusion, rather than asserting the group ring itself maximal. The locally fabricated RingK0 type, group and class map were deleted. Its downstream class-group and Fröhlich–Taylor specifications are a clearly labelled nested comment awaiting the actual KTheoryLowDegrees carrier and projectivity bridge. They are not executable declarations.

No new mathematical node was invented to disguise a missing proof. At lemma granularity, the continuation must split the bundled data and consumed API lemmas enumerated below, then provide direct prerequisites. Examples include the Smith/Hermite algorithms and invariants, analytic radius/pole arguments, ideal-lattice and class-group certificates, Dobrowolski auxiliary estimates, local trace/induction, and the root-number and cancellation inputs to Taylor. A broad stage reference cannot substitute for an exact supplier: T.7, QM.0, AN.2, CN.2, DY.1 and Z.1 still have source/interface obligations. The exact DT.0 height/Mahler contracts and FF.1 Stickelberger relation were read, as was Z.4’s rank/Picard equivalence.

## Baseline declaration audit

Pinned Mathlib commit: `082e2d37e8b0463410cdb532e111cd43d5a66174`. Pinned Tau Ceti commit: `f790474821cf4256814db967cb154e7af3d0c369`. The declaration index has SHA256 `86649a7d5f35d1178a45fe7aa4713741d03d43ff3b37bb8c91a1da1c794c8ce1` and was used only for validation, not as proof of statement adequacy. Each source statement, including its ambient assumptions, was read independently; generated aliases and ambiguous short names were checked in context.

`Ideal.smithCoeffs` is data for a nonzero ideal in an algebra with a chosen basis over a PID, not general rectangular Smith data. `Matrix.smith_normal_form_unique` does permit zero entries; the determinant-nonzero existence theorem does not supply rectangular existence. Corrections expose nonzero CRT moduli, the d=0 inverse-binomial case, ZMod0 conventions, nontrivial/primitive character hypotheses, odd-prime supplements and the distinction between field minpoly divisibility and integral-base divisibility.

Added support608 is `Nat.pow_eq_sum_stirlingSecond_mul_descFactorial`; it supplies the Stirling expansion, not a Hasse-product identity. Added support607 is `minpoly.isIntegrallyClosed_dvd`, including IsTorsionFree: it is used in the integral minimal-polynomial consumers. The table numbers are one-based; packet/scratch correction indices are zero-based. “Confirmed” means the actual cited declaration and revised provides contract exist; consumer-specific adaptation failures remain in the node verdicts, not silently certified here.

| Number | Declaration / pinned source | Verdict | Actual provides / consumers |

Voight’s author copy is v1.0.7u, August5,2026; it is distinct from the Springer2021 PDF and official errata. Publisher pages209,409 and the errata passage were collated only at the listed locators. The author’s ramified quadratic fibre is rank2/nonreduced, not k; the official replacement repeats that slip. The older publisher fibre description is different. Martin’s published2025 first page repairs the integer-tree wording (E501), but its Theorem1.4 still fails at p=3 (E508). Relyea v1,v2,v3 were compared at the quartic/power-sum locators only. Dimitrov’s Annals page announces a revision in June2026 and acceptance in August2026; the accepted proof was not obtained, so findings remain scoped to arXiv v1. Koley’s journal article was not collated. Bounded correction searches establish neither global novelty nor absence of any other erratum.

## Source-finding dispositions

All51 inherited entries have this review’s own verdict; E507’s previous verdict is preserved in its reviewHistory. E406 is rejected because independent source confirmation was impossible, not because the proposed correction was disproved. Every added entry is attributed to this review and confirmed against the stated public text. The correction column carries the exact repair; all findings remain edition-scoped.

| ID | Source / locator | Verdict | Correction and reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ClassicalArithmeticCompletion/E201 | shastri-reciprocity-2000 — §9, proof of Theorem 5, p. 182 (publisher's PDF of the proceedings) | confirmed | The first product runs over v \| a: ∏_{v\|a} ⟨a, b⟩_v ∏_{v\|b} ⟨a, b⟩_v ∏_{v\|n∞} ⟨a, b⟩_v ∏_{v∈V₁} ⟨a, b⟩_v. Independently read at §9, proof of Theorem 5, p. 182 (publisher's PDF of the proceedings) in the exact public version identified in the source ledger. V₀ = {v : v \| a b n ∞}, so the places dividing a must appear; the next lines of the proof evaluate ⟨a, b⟩_v 'if v \| a' and 'if v \| b', one for each of the first two products. |
| ClassicalArithmeticCompletion/E202 | shastri-reciprocity-2000 — §10, p. 183 | confirmed | (a/b)·(b/a) = (−1)^(((a−1)/2)·((b−1)/2)): the two exponents are multiplied, not the two signs. Independently read at §10, p. 183 in the exact public version identified in the source ledger. For a = 5, b = −1 the text itself computes ⟨5, −1⟩₂ = 1, while the printed right side is (−1)^2 (−1)^(−1) = −1; for a = b = −1, ⟨−1, −1⟩₂ = −1 while the printed right side is +1. The product of exponents gives 1 and −1, as required, and is the law of quadratic reciprocity the section derives. |
| ClassicalArithmeticCompletion/E203 | relyea-reciprocity-2024 — Theorem 4.4 (The Law of Biquadratic Reciprocity), p. 45, arXiv v3 | confirmed | χπ(λ) = χλ(π) · (−1)^(((Nλ−1)/4)·((Nπ−1)/4)), that is χπ(λ) χλ(π)^(−1) = (−1)^(((Nλ−1)/4)·((Nπ−1)/4)); the product of the two characters is not a sign in general. Independently read at Theorem 4.4 (The Law of Biquadratic Reciprocity), p. 45, arXiv v3 in the exact public version identified in the source ledger. In ℤ[i] take the primary primes π = −1 + 2i (norm 5) and λ = −3 (norm 9). Modulo π, i ≡ 3 and −3 ≡ 2 ≡ 3^3 = i^3, so χπ(λ) = −i; modulo 3, π^2 = −3 − 4i ≡ −i, so χλ(π) = −i. The product is (−i)^2 = −1, while the printed right side is (−1)^(2·1) = 1; the quotient is 1, as the corrected law requires. |
| ClassicalArithmeticCompletion/E204 | relyea-reciprocity-2024 — §4.1, Definition 22 and Theorem 4.4, p. 45, arXiv v3 | confirmed | Primary in ℤ[i] must be defined (α ≡ 1 modulo (1 + i)^3), and χλ must be extended multiplicatively to primary elements λ that are not irreducible (or the theorem restricted to primary primes). Independently read at §4.1, Definition 22 and Theorem 4.4, p. 45, arXiv v3 in the exact public version identified in the source ledger. Section 4 never defines primary for ℤ[i] (the definition in §3 is the Eisenstein one, π ≡ 2 mod 3), and χλ is defined only for irreducible λ, while the theorem quantifies over primary elements; the statement is not determined by the text. |
| ClassicalArithmeticCompletion/E205 | bennett-siksek-erdos-2020 — §7, Lemma 7.3, p. 375 (published version) | confirmed | Let N be the conductor of a quadratic character, ... Independently read at arXiv v1 §1 p.1; compare published 2025 text §1 p.623 / PDF1 in the exact public version identified in the source ledger.
